# Sync with upstream OfflineDAoC v0.37b: implementation plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** HearthDAoC runs on upstream OfflineDAoC v0.37b (the full 0.36b release plus upstream's 0.37 game patch),
with every fork feature, world fix and test working on the new world, so that merging the PR publishes
`v0.37b-hearth.1` and `./hdc update` takes the owner's 0.35b world there in one step.

**Architecture:** Task 1 recreates the trial's resolved merge of `v0.37b` on `sync/v0.37b` from the saved
resolutions. The deploy tasks teach the fetcher a lock made of a base release plus an upstream patch, port upstream's
world-update applier (strict, in one transaction) and build every new clean world (new world, `fetch-clean`,
`upgrade-world`, CI) as the cached 0.36b world plus the patch's world update. The other tasks adapt the fork's world
fixes, quest code and quest data to what 0.36 and 0.37 changed, each under its owner decision or ruling, and
re-baseline the world tests on the 0.37b world; the docs task brings FORK.md, HANDOFF.md and the player guide up to
date.

**Tech Stack:** C# (.NET 10, NUnit 4) for the server and its unit tests; Python 3 (`unittest`, `sqlite3`) for the
deploy scripts, world fixes and their tests; bash for the container tests; GitHub Actions CI
(`.github/workflows/server-image.yml`); Docker for the image.

**Spec:** issue #138 and its decisions comment (2026-10-18), plus
`/home/farral/Games/DAoC/research/upstream-037-2026-10-18/README.md` sections 4-6 and its strands in the same folder
(local research: `quests.md`, `bots-rvr.md`, `performance.md`, `deploy-world-client.md`, `other-server.md`,
`merge-build.md`, `world-check-quests.md`, `world-check-fixes.md`, `relic-pad-period.md`,
`pocket-escapes-2026-10-18.md`). **Owner change, 2026-10-18, after the README was written: decision 3, the relic pad,
is upstream's 8 attackers, with companions and gamebots counting. There is no pin to 1:** `relic-pad-pin.patch` is not
applied, and wherever the README (5.7, 6.2 steps 3 and 14, section 8), a strand or an older note says "pin 1", "no
minimum" or "a lone player takes an enemy relic", this plan follows the change instead.

**Where each decision and ruling lands** (#138's decisions 1-8; README 4's R1-R24):
- Decision 1 (GoS XP as shares, c): Tasks 16-17 (the `Merge` rule and its mirror), 22 (the data), 12 (the pay step
  accepts 0.36's RewardXP). Decision 2 (GoS items, a): Task 13. Decision 3 (relic pad, upstream's 8): Task 9.
  Decisions 4 and 5 (guilds, raid bosses: upstream's): no code, the merge (Task 1) brings them. Decision 6 (clean
  worlds, local only): Task 1 Step 7 copies them into the test data folder. Decision 7 (hotfix only on the freeze): no
  code; the log command of "After the merge" (the owner may also run it on hearth.20 before merging, README 7 step 2:
  a freeze there means the v0.35b-hearth.21 hotfix first). Decision 8 (sync first, then the CPU meter): this plan's
  order.
- R1 and R3: Task 1. R2: Task 2. R4: Task 3. R5: Tasks 10 (the commander), 24 (six Chat lines), 25 (three dialogue
  entries). R6, R21, R23: Task 22. R8: Task 4. R13: Tasks 7-8 (every upgrade imports into a fresh base-plus-patch
  world). R14: Tasks 8 (Review Focus 5) and 30. R19: Task 25. R20: Tasks 26-28. R22: Task 9. R24: "After the merge".
  No code: R7 (upstream's level-up marker refresh stays; Task 30 notes it), R9, R10, R17 (upstream's, as merged), R11
  and R12 (the CPU meter's, later), R15 (no captured keeps to carry), R18 (operational, until this release). **R16**
  (fork kill targets get the 300 s respawn) has no task: neither #138's work list nor README 6.2 has one, and no part
  of this plan changes a `RespawnInterval` (an open question for the owner).

Every code block below was built and tested in a scratch copy before the plan was written, task by task as the steps
say, and every `Expected:` is the output seen there. New tests: 20 C# cases of the fork's, upstream's 2
`UT_RelicPadAttackers` now in CI's filter, 87 deploy tests, 12 fetcher tests and 1 client test; at the end CI's fork
filter runs 630 (608 after Task 1), the whole C# project 3,418, and `deploy/tests` 715 tests, `OK (skipped=4)`, on the
0.37b world Task 29 builds. The tested commits are the local branch `sync/v0.37b-tested` of `~/github/HearthDAoC` (not pushed), head
`4b67c0e63da1fd66ba5fa1aaa46fc385aec21101`, on the trial's merge `8f4c939` (Task 1's commit; parents `487b95e` and
`ab4df02`): one commit per task, Tasks 7, 8, 13 and 25 two (their Review Focus test after their main commit), Tasks 15
and 29 none. The owner can choose to adopt those tested commits with one whole-branch review instead of having them
rebuilt from this plan; then the plan is the review guide. The plan itself was then replayed as written, from Task 1
in a fresh clone of `487b95e`, every block in a fresh shell from the repository root, with the helper and the test
data folder exactly as given here: every commit's tree equals the tested branch's commit of the same step, and the
`Expected:` blocks are that replay's output (run times vary; Task 29 Step 6's container tests ran once, on the tested
branch before its last two doc-text fixes). The plan review (2026-10-19) applied every file-changing block again (86
helper calls, 8 new files, the three generators) from `8f4c939`, after its own edits to this plan: the files of each
of the 31 commits equal the tested branch's byte for byte, and each commit message equals the plan's.

## Global Constraints

- Work in `~/github/HearthDAoC-sync`, branch `sync/v0.37b`, made from `origin/main` `487b95e` (v0.35b-hearth.20).
  Never commit to `main`.
- Nothing goes upstream (paused by the owner): never push to or open anything on `shadowofze/OfflineDAoC` or OpenDAoC.
- Implementers don't push or open PRs; the controller does at the end, with one PR that closes #138 (merging it
  publishes `v0.37b-hearth.1`).
- Implementers never dispatch subagents.
- Nobody contacts the owner's server (192.168.1.64); the in-game checks are the owner's.
- Never commit an EA file or a world: no world database, `game.dll`, client art or other EA file. World copies, the
  patch zip and the built CLIs live in the test data folder `~/tmp/hearthdaoc-sync037/td` (Task 1 Step 7), outside the repository.
- Every commit message is `type(sync): a plain lower-case sentence`, a wrapped prose body, and ends with the line
  `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>` (Task 1's merge keeps the trial's merge message, with the
  same last line). Every git command runs with `LC_ALL=C`.
- **This plan file stays untracked until the end.** It is `docs/fork/plans/2026-10-18-sync-v0.37b.md` in the worktree,
  and no task adds it: Task 1's merge must have the trial's exact tree, and every later commit's tree is the tested
  branch's. So every `git status --short` below also lists `?? docs/fork/plans/2026-10-18-sync-v0.37b.md`, which the
  `Expected:` blocks leave out (Task 1 Step 1's count skips it); every task adds its files by name. The controller
  commits the plan after Task 30, on its own (`docs(sync): the plan of the sync to upstream 0.37b`), before the push.
- **Line endings and BOMs stay exactly as they are.** Edit existing files **only** with the helper below; `git diff`
  must show only your lines (the one exception: Part 5's two generators and the fork's own `quest_dialogue.py --seal`
  write their fork data files whole, LF without a BOM, which their steps check). It keeps each file's BOM and each
  line's own ending, so it also edits upstream's `ClassicQuests.cs` (14 CRLF lines in an LF file, `ScaleXP`) without
  converting a line, and with `--inline` it changes text inside one long line (`deploy/hearthdaoc-quests.json`'s
  `"_about"`). Create it with the Write tool exactly as below (or replace an older copy: an earlier helper may already
  be at that path, and it knows neither `--inline` nor mixed endings), once, at `/tmp/hdc-edit.py`, outside the
  repository, before Task 1; Task 1 Step 1 checks its SHA-256 (`c34f7b67…4a9f`):

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Replaces text in one file, keeping its BOM and each line's ending (CRLF or LF).

Usage: python3 /tmp/hdc-edit.py [--inline] FILE <<'EDIT' ... EDIT
The input holds one or more blocks, each:
<<<<<<< OLD
the old text (LF line ends; matched as written, and as CRLF lines when the file has any)
=======
the new text (written with the line ends of the lines it replaces)
>>>>>>> NEW
Each old text must occur exactly once in the file, as LF or as CRLF lines; otherwise nothing is written and the run
stops. A file of mixed endings (upstream's ClassicQuests.cs: 14 CRLF lines in an LF file) keeps them: a block matches
the lines it was written for and its new text takes their ending. With --inline, each block's old and new text lose
their last line end, so a block can change text inside one long line (deploy/hearthdaoc-quests.json's "_about").
"""
import sys

args = sys.argv[1:]
inline = args[:1] == ["--inline"]
if inline:
    args = args[1:]
if len(args) != 1:
    sys.exit("usage: hdc-edit.py [--inline] FILE")
path = args[0]
with open(path, "rb") as f:
    raw = f.read()
blocks = []
for part in sys.stdin.read().split("<<<<<<< OLD\n")[1:]:
    old, rest = part.split("=======\n", 1)
    new = rest.split(">>>>>>> NEW\n", 1)[0]
    if inline:
        old, new = old[:-1] if old.endswith("\n") else old, new[:-1] if new.endswith("\n") else new
    blocks.append((old, new))
if not blocks:
    sys.exit("no edit blocks")
for old, new in blocks:
    o, n = old.encode("utf-8"), new.encode("utf-8")
    forms = [(o, n)]
    if b"\r\n" in raw and b"\n" in o:
        forms.append((o.replace(b"\n", b"\r\n"), n.replace(b"\n", b"\r\n")))
    found = [(fo, fn) for fo, fn in forms if raw.count(fo)]
    count = sum(raw.count(fo) for fo, _ in forms)
    if count != 1:
        sys.exit(f"{path}: old text found {count} times: {old[:80]!r}")
    fo, fn = found[0]
    raw = raw.replace(fo, fn)
with open(path, "wb") as f:
    f.write(raw)
print(f"edited {path} ({len(blocks)} block(s))")
```

- New fork files are LF, no BOM, indented with spaces, C# with a file-scoped namespace, like
  `source/server/GameServer/scripts/hearthdaoc/RpTimerWait.cs`. Create them with the Write tool or a quoted heredoc
  exactly as given.
- Every edit inside an upstream file sits under a `// HearthDAoC:` comment (`# HearthDAoC:` in Python or shell), as
  small as possible: a one-line hook into fork code when there is logic. Count the marks on tracked files (a plain
  `grep -r` also counts build output) with
  `LC_ALL=C git grep -c '// HearthDAoC:' -- source/server | awk -F: '{s+=$NF; n++} END {print s " in " n " files"}'`
  and `LC_ALL=C git grep -c 'HearthDAoC:' -- source/ | awk -F: '{s+=$NF; n++} END {print s " in " n " files"}'`:
  120 in 56 and 140 in 60 after Task 1; 130 in 59 and 147 in 59 after the last task (Task 3: -3 of any style, +1
  `//`; Tasks 16-21: +10 and +9).
- TDD: write the failing test first, run it and see it fail for the reason the step names, then the code, then see
  it pass. A Review Focus step adds a test for behaviour that already exists; it shows the test biting on a broken
  copy instead, then restores the file.
- A new C# unit test class goes into CI's fork filter in `.github/workflows/server-image.yml` and into
  `deploy/tests/test_workflows.py` (`SERVER_UNIT_TESTS` and its names list), after the last class there. This plan's
  only addition is upstream's `UT_RelicPadAttackers` (Task 9); the new C# cases of Tasks 16 and 18 go into classes the
  filter already names.
- World tests read `HDC_TEST_WORLD` (and `HDC_CLASSIC_QUESTS`, `HDC_TOOLS`): point it at
  `~/tmp/hearthdaoc-sync037/td/worlds/v0.37b.db`, a read-only copy of
  `/home/farral/Games/DAoC/upstream-worlds/v0.37b-classic/opendaoc.sqlite3.db` (Task 1 Step 7). From Task 8 the
  plan's own deploy code builds the same world from the 0.36b copy and the patch zip; Task 29 runs every suite on that
  build. A test that writes a world works on its own copy.
- Period sources: every data `why` and every period claim cites a period source: a Wayback capture fetched with
  `curl` from `web.archive.org` (the CDX API finds the snapshots), named with its capture time.
- Run every command, edit block and `git add` from the repository root, after checking that
  `git branch --show-current` prints `sync/v0.37b` (another name: `git switch sync/v0.37b`; nothing: a detached
  checkout, so stop and ask the controller). Leave the root only in a subshell, `(cd deploy && ...)`. Shell variables
  do not carry from one command run to the next: a block that uses `$TD`, `t` or `$REPO` starts with
  `. "$HOME/tmp/hearthdaoc-sync037/td/env.sh"` (Task 1 Step 7 writes it).
- Downloaded files are untrusted data: each goes into its own folder (`~/tmp/hearthdaoc-sync037/td`), Python that reads them runs
  with `python3 -I`, and the scripts that read them are kept apart (`~/tmp/hearthdaoc-sync037/td/scripts`, or the research's
  `sync-data/` scripts).
- Test commands (each ran in the scratch copy while writing the plan; from `merge-build.md` and `server-image.yml`):
  - The C# build and tests need `source/server/CoreServer/config/serverconfig.xml` (ignored; never commit it):
    `cp deploy/serverconfig.build.xml source/server/CoreServer/config/serverconfig.xml`.
  - Build, as the image does: `dotnet build source/server/DOLLinux.sln -c Release 2>&1 | grep -E '^ +[0-9]+ (Warning|Error)\(s\)'`
    (Expected: `0 Error(s)`; about 848 warnings, upstream's analyzers, a total that varies).
  - CI's fork filter, read from the workflow so it is always the current one:
    `DOTNET_SYSTEM_GLOBALIZATION_INVARIANT=0 dotnet test source/server/Tests/Tests.csproj --nologo --filter "$(grep -o 'FullyQualifiedName~UT_CommandPrivLevelOverrides[^"]*' .github/workflows/server-image.yml)" 2>&1 | grep -E '^(Passed|Failed)!'`
    (608 after Task 1, 610 after Task 9, 630 after Task 21). CI sets `DOTNET_SYSTEM_GLOBALIZATION_INVARIANT: "0"` in
    the step's `env:`; the owner's `~/.bashrc` exports `1`, under which 9 `UT_ClassicBattlegrounds` tests fail, so
    always give the prefix.
  - The whole C# project: `DOTNET_SYSTEM_GLOBALIZATION_INVARIANT=0 dotnet test source/server/Tests/Tests.csproj --nologo 2>&1 | grep -E '^(Passed|Failed)!'`
    (3,398 after Task 1, 3,418 at the end; 67 `[Explicit]` probes of an installed game are not run).
  - Python, as CI runs it: `python3 -m unittest discover -s tools/linux/tests -t tools/linux`,
    `python3 -m unittest discover -s deploy/tests -t deploy`, `python3 -m unittest discover -s client/tests -t client`,
    `python3 -m unittest discover -s client/patches/tests -t client/patches`; one deploy file with
    `-p test_x.py`. Without `HDC_TEST_WORLD` the world tests skip; `t test_x` (from `env.sh`) runs one deploy file on
    the 0.37b copy with the 0.37 `classic-quests.json` and the built CLIs (`WORLD=... t test_x` for another world).
  - Upstream's `PooledWalReadersAndWriters_CanOpenConnectionsConcurrently` can fail with "database is locked" when the
    machine is busy: run the suites one after the other; if it fails, run it again alone and say so.

## Review Focus

The five input classes or failure modes most likely to bite the owner or the players that no task's own tests
exercised, most likely first. Each now has a test in the task that owns it (tested in the scratch copy):

1. **CI's container tests pin start-log lines the 0.37b world changes.** `smoke.sh` and `hdc_integration.sh` waited
   for "Quest dialogue: 145 quests rewritten" (0.35b's count; the 0.37b world prints 142), and no task runs them, so
   the PR's first CI run would fail at "Smoke test". Test: `deploy/tests/test_container_lines.py` (Task 25 Step 6),
   with both scripts re-baselined.
2. **The image's first start through the real lock** (GitHub range reads of the 0.36b parts and the 0.37 patch, the
   world update, the server files, the server on the 0.37b world): only CI's image steps do it, after the push. Test:
   Task 29 Step 6 builds the image and runs `smoke.sh` and `hdc_integration.sh` locally.
3. **A Guild of Shadows reward a player holds, on the owner's upgraded world.** Every world test used a clean world;
   the owner's is a clean 0.37b world plus imported Inventory rows, whose templates the first start replaces
   (decision 2.a). Test: `deploy/tests/test_gos_held_items.py` (Task 13 Step 6).
4. **The owner's hearth.20 navmesh folder on the first start after `./hdc update`.** Without the patch's
   `pockets.json` upstream's pocket fixes are silently off, and re-reading the 103 zone meshes costs the download
   HANDOFF.md says is not needed. Test: `test_the_start_after_an_upgrade_adds_the_patchs_navmesh_files_and_keeps_the_zone_meshes`
   (Task 7 Step 6).
5. **The players' client patches against the new lock.** The classic edition's `game.dll` now comes from another
   folder of another release; a pin that no longer matches leaves every player's client unpatched ("Not patched"),
   and CI's rebuild check (which needs the EA files) is the only guard. Test:
   `LockTests.test_the_client_patch_sets_patch_the_releases_own_client_files` and
   `RealPatchTests.test_the_patch_replaces_no_client_file_the_patch_sets_patch` (Task 8 Step 6).

### Task 1: The merge of v0.37b, from the trial's saved resolutions (R1, R3)

**Files:**
- Merge: upstream `v0.37b` (`ab4df02`) into `sync/v0.37b` (`487b95e`): 22 conflicts, 9 content and 13 modify/delete
- Copy: the 11 resolved files of `/home/farral/Games/DAoC/research/upstream-037-2026-10-18/trial/resolved/`
  (`README.md`, `AutonomousBotGroupCoordinator.cs`, `AutonomousWorldBotController.Battleground.cs`,
  `ClassicQuests.cs`, `DataQuest.cs`, `QuestDependencies.cs`, the four epic-50 scripts, `deploy/tests/test_rp_timer_wait.py`)
- Remove: `source/server/GameServer/scripts/quests/Albion/epic/Shadows50.cs` and the 12
  `source/server/GameServer/scripts/quests/BattlegroundQuests/{Caledonia,Thidranki}/*.cs` (R3, #76)
- Create (outside the repository): `~/tmp/hearthdaoc-sync037/td/` (the test data) and `~/tmp/hearthdaoc-sync037/td/env.sh`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the tag `v0.37b` (`ab4df02a1ad26192357477dbc912756c6703fb66`) in the repository; the saved resolutions
  (`merge-build.md` section 1 says how each was made); the clean worlds in `/home/farral/Games/DAoC/upstream-worlds/`
  and upstream's `OfflineDAoC-v0.37-game-patch.zip` (38,084,367 bytes, SHA-256 `48fd6482…69c1`).
- Produces: the merge commit, with the trial's exact message, tree `dc3b8f8ebc66d72d53665e2b5f0d911bf301f1cf` and
  parents `487b95e` and `ab4df02` (the tested branch's base, `8f4c939`); the marks of the merge (120 `// HearthDAoC:` in
  56 files, 140 of any style in 60); `~/tmp/hearthdaoc-sync037/td/` with `OfflineDAoC-v0.37-game-patch.zip`, `worlds/v0.35b.db`,
  `worlds/v0.36b.db`, `worlds/v0.37b.db` (read-only copies), `q037/classic-quests.json` (upstream's 0.37 file, from
  the patch zip), `tools/` (the CLIs built from the merged tree: `bot-goals`, `offline-bots`, `progress-import`, the
  0.37 import engine) and `env.sh` (`TD`, `REPO` and `t`).

The trial merged `v0.37b` into `487b95e`, resolved every conflict, built with 0 errors and passed 3,398 C# tests and
every Python suite (README 5.1-5.2, `merge-build.md`). R1 adopts those resolutions as they were saved, so this task
makes the same merge again: the conflicts, then each resolved file copied over, the 13 deleted files kept deleted, and
a check that the index is the trial's byte for byte. The group coordinator is upstream's new CRLF file plus our four
marked lines; the battleground controller runs upstream's `tooFew` check, our realm-point wait, then upstream's field
fight; `ClassicQuests.cs` and `DataQuest.cs` keep both sides; the epic 50s keep our lookups (Task 3 changes three);
`test_rp_timer_wait.py` carries the trial's two test-text fixes (`:249` upstream's new `_rvrIntent` line, `:299`
the coordinator's CRLF).

These tasks finish what the merge leaves (`merge-build.md` section 6): the endings checks (R2), the level-50 lookups
(R4) and the siege release (R8). The relic pad is not here: decision 3 is upstream's 8 with companions and gamebots
counting, so nothing pins it and `relic-pad-pin.patch` is not applied.

`HearthDAoC:` of any style in 60 files under `source/` after Task 1; 121 in 57 and 137 in 57 after Task 3 (Academy50,

`// HearthDAoC:` line). Tasks 2 and 4 change no mark.

### Task 2: The endings checks want one ending per file (R2)

**Files:**
- Modify: `deploy/tests/test_rp_timer_wait.py:65` (a new `ending` helper before `cs_files`), `:297-303`
  (`EndingsTests.test_the_upstream_files_keep_their_endings`)
- Modify: `deploy/tests/test_quest_indicators.py:12-14` (the import), `:97-105`
  (`EndingsTests.test_the_upstream_files_keep_their_endings_and_bom`)
- Test: the same two files

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the path constants `BG`, `WBC`, `GC`, `FT` of `test_rp_timer_wait.py` and `GAME_NPC` ... `MONTHLY` of
  `test_quest_indicators.py` (unchanged).
- Produces: `ending(raw: bytes) -> "CRLF" | "LF" | None` in `deploy/tests/test_rp_timer_wait.py`, importable as
  `from tests.test_rp_timer_wait import ending` under `-t deploy`; None for a file that mixes CRLF and LF or holds a
  lone CR. Test methods `EndingsTests.test_the_upstream_files_keep_one_ending` and
  `EndingsTests.test_a_file_of_both_endings_fails` (test_rp_timer_wait), and
  `EndingsTests.test_the_upstream_files_keep_one_ending_and_their_bom` (test_quest_indicators). The BOM checks are
  unchanged.

Upstream turned `AutonomousBotGroupCoordinator.cs` from LF to CRLF in 0.36 and broke the fixed list the trial had to
patch; its endings keep flipping (22 server files from 0.35b to 0.37b). The fork takes upstream's endings (R2), so
the checks accept either ending and still fail a file of both, which is what a fork line in the wrong ending makes.

```bash
def ending(raw):
    """A file's line ending, "CRLF" or "LF" (also for a file of one line), or None for a file of both or a lone CR."""
    lf, crlf = raw.count(b"\n"), raw.count(b"\r\n")
    if raw.count(b"\r") != crlf:
        return None
    if crlf == 0:
        return "LF"
    return "CRLF" if crlf == lf else None
```

### Task 3: The level-50 lookups take upstream's `IsAt`, and Mystic50 keeps both boxes (R4)

**Files:**
- Modify: `source/server/GameServer/scripts/quests/Albion/epic/Academy50.cs:114`, `source/server/GameServer/scripts/quests/Hibernia/epic/Essence50.cs:118`,
  `source/server/GameServer/scripts/quests/Midgard/epic/Mystic50.cs:123`, `source/server/GameServer/scripts/quests/Midgard/epic/Viking50.cs:169` (CRLF upstream files; the helper keeps
  CRLF)
- Modify: `deploy/tests/test_epic_chains.py:125-157` (`LOOKUPS`, the lookup helper, `EpicLookupWorldTests`),
  `:166-171` (`EpicSourceTests.test_each_level_50_quest_finds_its_npc_near_where_it_creates_it`)
- Test: `deploy/tests/test_epic_chains.py` (`EpicSourceTests`; `EpicLookupWorldTests` on a copy of the 0.37b
  classic world)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: upstream's `DOL.GS.Quests.QuestNpcPlacement.IsAt(GameNPC npc, ushort region, int x, int y)` and
  `QuestNpcPlacement.SearchRadius` (3000), `source/server/GameServer/quests/QuestsMgr/QuestNpcPlacement.cs`; Task 2's
  tree.
- Produces: in `deploy/tests/test_epic_chains.py`: `PLACEMENT` (the path of `QuestNpcPlacement.cs`),
  `DANICA_UPSTREAM = (100, 802818, 727413)`, `search_radius() -> int`, `lookup(text, name, var) -> (line above,
  test line, [(region, x, y), ...], (region, x, y) created)`, `in_a_box(boxes, region, x, y) -> bool`
  (`lookup_and_creation` is gone); the tests `test_the_lookups_are_upstreams_but_danicas_second_box`,
  `test_is_at_is_a_box_of_search_radius_in_the_region` and
  `EpicLookupWorldTests.test_upstreams_box_alone_misses_the_worlds_danica`. Marks: 121 `// HearthDAoC:` in 57
  files, 137 `HearthDAoC:` in 57 files.

Upstream 0.36 fixed the same twin bug as the fork with a 3,000-unit box around each script's lookup spot. Ferowl's,
Brigit's and Elizabeth's creation spots and the world's Brigit and Elizabeth are inside upstream's boxes, so those
three files become upstream's again. The world's Danica (Mob `1b846a57-…`, region 100, 804440, 722267, the same in
all three clean worlds) is 1,622 / 5,146 units from Mystic50's lookup spot, outside its box, so Mystic50 also accepts
her within `SearchRadius` of its creation spot, 803559, 723329 (world-check-quests.md 4.1; quests.md, "The four epic
`*50.cs` files").

The world tests read `HDC_TEST_WORLD`: `$TD/worlds/v0.37b.db`, Task 1's copy of the clean 0.37b classic world
(`a0ffb014…cb40`).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```bash
python3 /tmp/hdc-edit.py deploy/tests/test_epic_chains.py <<'EDIT'

# Each level-50 quest looks its NPC up at a spot and creates it at another when none is found there, so a copy a GM
# saved shows up as a second NPC at every start (spec 3.3). (file, NPC name, variable the quest creates it in)
LOOKUPS = (
    ("scripts/quests/Albion/epic/Academy50.cs", "Master Ferowl", "Ferowl"),
    ("scripts/quests/Hibernia/epic/Essence50.cs", "Brigit", "Brigit"),
    ("scripts/quests/Midgard/epic/Mystic50.cs", "Danica", "Danica"),
    ("scripts/quests/Midgard/epic/Viking50.cs", "Elizabeth", "Elizabeth"),
)


def lookup_and_creation(text, name, var):
    """((lookup X, Y), (creation X, Y), (radius X, Y)) of the NPC's block in a level-50 quest."""
    block = text[text.index(f'GetNPCsByName("{name}"'):]
    found = re.search(r"Math\.Abs\(npc\.X - (\d+)\) <= (\d+) && Math\.Abs\(npc\.Y - (\d+)\) <= (\d+)", block)
    x = re.search(rf"\b{var}\.X = (\d+);", block)
    y = re.search(rf"\b{var}\.Y = (\d+);", block)
    return (found.group(1), found.group(3)), (x.group(1), y.group(1)), (found.group(2), found.group(4))


@unittest.skipUnless(TEST_WORLD, "needs HDC_TEST_WORLD (a clean classic world database)")
class EpicLookupWorldTests(unittest.TestCase):
    # The world's own copies of the level-50 NPCs are within the quests' lookup box, so the quests use them.
    def test_the_worlds_npcs_are_found_by_their_quests(self):
        conn = sqlite3.connect(f"file:{TEST_WORLD}?mode=ro", uri=True)
        try:
            for rel, name, var in LOOKUPS:
                (cx, cy), _made, (rx, ry) = lookup_and_creation(read(os.path.join(GAME_SERVER, rel)), name, var)
                for x, y in conn.execute("SELECT X, Y FROM Mob WHERE Name=?", (name,)):
                    with self.subTest(name=name):
                        self.assertLessEqual(abs(x - int(cx)), int(rx))
                        self.assertLessEqual(abs(y - int(cy)), int(ry))
        finally:
            conn.close()
=======
# Each level-50 quest looks its NPC up and creates it when none is found, so a copy a GM saved showed up as a second NPC
# at every start (spec 3.3). Upstream 0.36's QuestNpcPlacement.IsAt accepts one within SearchRadius (X and Y) of the
# lookup spot; Mystic50 also accepts Danica within it of the spot where it creates her, since the world's Danica stands
# outside upstream's box. (file, NPC name, variable the quest creates it in)
LOOKUPS = (
    ("scripts/quests/Albion/epic/Academy50.cs", "Master Ferowl", "Ferowl"),
    ("scripts/quests/Hibernia/epic/Essence50.cs", "Brigit", "Brigit"),
    ("scripts/quests/Midgard/epic/Mystic50.cs", "Danica", "Danica"),
    ("scripts/quests/Midgard/epic/Viking50.cs", "Elizabeth", "Elizabeth"),
)
PLACEMENT = os.path.join(GAME_SERVER, "quests", "QuestsMgr", "QuestNpcPlacement.cs")
DANICA_UPSTREAM = (100, 802818, 727413)  # upstream's lookup spot in Mystic50


def search_radius():
    """QuestNpcPlacement.SearchRadius: IsAt's box reaches this far from its spot in X and in Y."""
    return int(re.search(r"public const int SearchRadius = (\d+);", read(PLACEMENT)).group(1))


def lookup(text, name, var):
    """(the line above the NPC's lookup test, the test line, its IsAt boxes as (region, X, Y), the spot where the
    quest creates the NPC as (region, X, Y)) in a level-50 quest."""
    block = text[text.index(f'GetNPCsByName("{name}"'):]
    lines = block[block.index("foreach (GameNPC npc in npcs)"):].split("\n")
    at = next(i for i, line in enumerate(lines) if line.strip().startswith("if ("))
    boxes = [tuple(int(v) for v in m.groups())
             for m in re.finditer(r"QuestNpcPlacement\.IsAt\(npc, (\d+), (\d+), (\d+)\)", lines[at])]
    made = tuple(int(re.search(rf"\b{var}\.{field} = (\d+);", block).group(1)) for field in ("CurrentRegionID", "X", "Y"))
    return lines[at - 1], lines[at], boxes, made


def in_a_box(boxes, region, x, y):
    return any(r == region and abs(x - bx) <= search_radius() and abs(y - by) <= search_radius() for r, bx, by in boxes)


@unittest.skipUnless(TEST_WORLD, "needs HDC_TEST_WORLD (a clean classic world database)")
```

```bash
fix(sync): the level-50 lookups take upstream's box, and Danica keeps a second one

Upstream 0.36 fixed the same twin bug as the fork with QuestNpcPlacement.IsAt, a 3,000-unit box around each script's
lookup spot, so Academy50, Essence50 and Viking50 drop the fork's 2,000-unit box around the creation spot and are
upstream's files again. The world's Danica stands 1,622 and 5,146 units from Mystic50's lookup spot, outside
upstream's box, so Mystic50 also accepts her within the same box around the spot where it creates her (ruling R4,
one marked line). The source checks read IsAt and SearchRadius, and a world check says why the second box is still
needed.
```

### Task 4: A released siege attacker waits at its frontier muster (R8)

**Files:**
- Modify: `deploy/tests/test_rp_timer_wait.py:38` (two path constants), `:246-249` (a new
  `UpstreamFactTests` test after `test_the_force_key_and_the_formation_point_are_upstreams`), `:347` (a new
  `GuideTests` test)
- Modify: `docs/fork/verification/rp-timer-waits-ingame.md:10-15` (the opening), `:130-133` (check 7)
- Modify: `docs/fork/specs/2026-10-13-rp-timer-waits-design.md:180-181` (a line after the last departure)
- Test: `deploy/tests/test_rp_timer_wait.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `between`, `source`, `GuideTests.GUIDE` of `test_rp_timer_wait.py`; upstream's release order in
  `GameBot.cs` (`bots/GameBot.cs:1100-1105` at the merge: bind, raid hub, then
  `AutonomousRvrEventLayer.TrySiegeRelease`, then the capital) and `TrySiegeRelease` asking
  `AutonomousRvrStaging.TryGetSiegeMuster(realm, target.RegionId, ...)`.
- Produces: constants `EVENT_LAYER`, `GAME_BOT`; tests
  `UpstreamFactTests.test_a_released_siege_attacker_comes_back_at_its_frontier_muster` and
  `GuideTests.test_check_7_has_siege_attackers_wait_at_their_frontier_muster`. No code change: the wait's rule
  already lets a siege member wait (bots-rvr.md 5.2).

From 0.37 a released attacker of a running siege stands at its realm's portal outpost in the keep's region, so the
wait's first look finds it in the region of its fight: it waits there (`start case=siege`) or skips with
`reason=trip` when the walk is longer than its time left; with a healer of its army near it now lies 240 s before
release, so most skip. Solo invaders still release in their capital (`other-region`).

```bash
What changes: a gamebot killed in RvR or a battleground and released waits at a safe spot (its realm's arrival point in
a battleground, its bind in the frontier, or its warband's slot at the border keep). It leaves when its time left is no
more than its trip back, so it arrives just as it is worth realm points again, 300 s after its last death. The timer
itself is unchanged. A bot resurrected where it fell fights on. Waiting bots rest and rebuff and fight back when they
or a realm-mate near them are attacked, but don't run at enemies they only see. In this stage every battleground bot
leaves at its own moment (no waves).
=======

there, or its warband's slot at the border keep). It leaves when its time left is no more than its trip back, so it
arrives just as it is worth realm points again, 300 s after its last death. The timer itself is unchanged. A bot
resurrected where it fell fights on. Waiting bots rest and rebuff and fight back when they or a realm-mate near them
are attacked, but don't run at enemies they only see. In this stage every battleground bot leaves at its own moment
(no waves).

- **Siege attackers wait at their frontier muster (added 2026-10-18, the sync to upstream 0.37b).** Upstream 0.37
  releases an attacker of a running siege that has no bind of its realm nearby at its realm's muster for the keep's
  frontier, the portal outpost where its frontier teleporter lands, not in its capital (`bots/GameBot.cs:1100-1105`,
  `AutonomousRvrEventLayer.TrySiegeRelease`, `AutonomousRvrStaging.TryGetSiegeMuster`, at the merge). Its first look
  is then in the region of its fight, so it waits there as a siege member (3.3, `start case=siege`), or goes at once
  when the walk to the keep is longer than its time left (`skip reason=trip`): most do, since with a healer of its
  army near it now lies 240 s before it releases (`bots/GameBot.cs:1000-1003`). No code changes: the rule already
  lets a siege member wait. Where this spec says attackers of a keep in an enemy frontier release in their capital and
  never wait (the bullet "Only bots released in the region of their fight wait", 2.5's "Siege attacker" row, 3.3,
```

```bash
to the keep is longer than its time left (ruling R8). The in-game guide's check 7 and its opening say so, and the
```

## Part 2: Deploy pipeline, a base release plus an upstream patch (Tasks 5-8)

Upstream ships 0.37b as the full 0.36b game plus `OfflineDAoC-v0.37-game-patch.zip` (README 5.4, 6.2 step 2;
`deploy-world-client.md` 1-5 and 7). These four tasks teach the fetcher a lock with a `patch` object, port upstream's
world-update applier, build a patched release's clean world (new world, `fetch-clean`, `upgrade-world`, CI) and pin
0.37b in `deploy/upstream.lock`.

- Every file touched is a fork file (`deploy/`, `tools/linux/`, `client/tests/`, `.github/workflows/`), so no
  `// HearthDAoC:` mark is needed and the mark counts stay as Task 3 left them. No C# test class is added, so CI's fork
  filter (`server-image.yml:116` after Task 1, `:119` after Task 7) is unchanged. All files touched are LF without a
  BOM, as before.
- `deploy/hdc` needs no change (it is byte-identical since 487b95e, `lock_version` at `deploy/hdc:81`): `./hdc update`
  reads the new lock's top-level `"version"` (0.37b), finds the 0.35b world and runs the new release's
  `upgrade-world`, whose `fetch-clean` now builds the 0.37b world (Task 7). Task 8 runs that path with upstream's
  real 0.37 import engine on a 0.35b world.
- Relic pad: nothing in this part (Task 9 checks that every world keeps upstream's 8).
- Real-data checks (Task 8) read copies of the clean worlds and the 0.37 patch zip from `~/tmp/hearthdaoc-sync037/td`, never
  commit them, and skip without their environment variables (as in CI).

### Task 5: Fetcher: a release made of a base and an upstream patch

**Files:**
- Modify: `tools/linux/odaoc_fetch.py:8 (insert after), 27 (insert after), 58-64, 67-68, 73-78, 171-190, 193, 223, 228 (insert after), 229 (insert after), 240, 244, 259, 265, 293`
- Modify: `tools/linux/tests/release_fixture.py:1-2, 5 (insert after), 11 (insert after), 33 (insert after), 82 (insert after), 130, 132 (insert after)`
- Test: `tools/linux/tests/test_odaoc_fetch.py:149 (insert after)`
- Test: `client/tests/test_setup.py:255 (insert after)`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the lock format of `deploy/upstream.lock` after Task 1 (top level: `version`, `root_folder`, `parts`
  [`url`, `size`], `manifest_path`, `manifest_sha256`, prefixes, `editions`); `release_fixture.build()` and
  `RangeServer`.
- Produces: in `odaoc_fetch.py`: `BASE_ONLY_PREFIXES = ("editions/",)`; `class Archive(urls, sizes, root, name,
  opener=None, retries=4, backoff=1.0)` with `read`, `entries()`, `_extract_entry(rel, dest, sha_expected,
  against="the release manifest")`; `class Release(Archive)` with `Release(lock, opener=None, retries=4, backoff=1.0,
  manifest_file=None)`, `patch()` (None, or `{"meta": patch.json, "zip": Archive, "files": {rel: patch.json
  entry}, "removed": set}`), `world_update(edition)` (None, or `(patch.json "Version", parsed update dict)`),
  `entry(rel)`, and patch-aware `expected_sha256(rel)`, `extract(rel, dest)`, `files_under(prefix)`; `fetch_client`
  and the CLI `list` use `entry()`. The lock's optional `patch` object: `url`, `size`, `sha256`, `root_folder`,
  `patch_json_sha256`, `world_updates` = `{lock edition: {"database": patch.json "Database" key, "path": file at
  the zip root, "sha256": hex}}`; no key named `version` inside it. Messages: `SHA-256 mismatch against the
  patch's patch.json: <rel>`, `SHA-256 mismatch against the lock's pin: patch.json`, `<rel> is removed by the
  <version> patch`, `the <version> patch is not for this base release: ...`, `... which only the base may hold`.
  In `release_fixture.py`: `PATCH_ROOT`, `PATCH_NAME = "game-patch.zip"`, `IDENTITY`, `build_patch(directory, lock,
  base_files, files=None, remove=(), updates=None, version="test.1", identity_from=None, tamper=None)` returning
  `(lock with "patch", {rel: bytes})`, and `RangeServer.lock()` pointing `lock["patch"]["url"]` at the server.

```bash
def build_patch(directory, lock, base_files, files=None, remove=(), updates=None, version="test.1",
                identity_from=None, tamper=None):
    """Write directory/parts/game-patch.zip, a fake upstream game patch on the release build() made, and
    return (lock with its "patch" object, {rel: bytes} of the patch's files).

    files: {rel: bytes} the patch adds or replaces (under files/ in the zip, listed in patch.json "Files").
    remove: rels patch.json "Remove" lists.
    updates: {lock edition: (patch.json "Database" key, file name, world update dict)}, at the zip's root.
    identity_from: the hashes patch.json says its Identity file may have (default: the base's).
    tamper: {rel: wrong_sha} written into patch.json instead of the file's real hash.
    """
    files = dict(files or {})
    tamper = tamper or {}
    updates = updates or {}
    meta = {
        "Version": version, "Kind": "game", "Title": f"test {version} update",
        "Identity": {"Path": IDENTITY, "To": _sha(b"new identity"),
                     "From": [_sha(base_files[IDENTITY])] if identity_from is None else identity_from},
        "Files": [{"Path": rel, "Sha256": tamper.get(rel, _sha(data)), "Bytes": len(data),
                   "From": [_sha(base_files[rel])] if rel in base_files else []}
                  for rel, data in sorted(files.items())],
        "Remove": [{"Path": rel, "From": [_sha(base_files[rel])]} for rel in remove],
        "Database": {key: name for key, name, _ in updates.values()},
    }
    meta_bytes = json.dumps(meta, indent=1).encode()
    update_bytes = {name: json.dumps(update).encode() for _, name, update in updates.values()}
    buf = io.BytesIO()
    with zipfile.ZipFile(buf, "w", zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED) as z:
        z.writestr(PATCH_ROOT, b"")  # directory entry, must be ignored
        z.writestr(PATCH_ROOT + "patch.json", meta_bytes)
        for name, data in sorted(update_bytes.items()):
            z.writestr(PATCH_ROOT + name, data)
        for rel, data in sorted(files.items()):
            z.writestr(PATCH_ROOT + "files/" + rel, data)
    blob = buf.getvalue()
    parts_dir = os.path.join(directory, "parts")
    os.makedirs(parts_dir, exist_ok=True)
    with open(os.path.join(parts_dir, PATCH_NAME), "wb") as f:
        f.write(blob)
    patch = {"name": PATCH_NAME, "size": len(blob), "sha256": _sha(blob), "root_folder": PATCH_ROOT,
             "patch_json_sha256": _sha(meta_bytes),
             "world_updates": {edition: {"database": key, "path": name, "sha256": _sha(update_bytes[name])}
                               for edition, (key, name, _) in updates.items()}}
    return dict(lock, patch=patch), files
```

```bash
GUIDES = "runtime/server/classic-quest-guides.json"

CLASSIC_DB = "editions/0.34-no-custom-class/runtime/data/opendaoc.sqlite3.db"

class PatchFetchTests(unittest.TestCase):
    """A lock with a "patch": upstream's game patch on a full base release (0.37b = 0.36b + the 0.37 patch)."""

    def setUp(self):
        self.tmp = tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()
        self.dir = self.tmp.name
        self.base_lock, self.base_files = fx.build(self.dir)

    def tearDown(self):
        self.tmp.cleanup()

    def patched(self, **kw):
        kw.setdefault("files", {QUESTS: b'{"Quests": {"new": 1}}', POCKETS: b'{"pockets": []}'})
        return fx.build_patch(self.dir, self.base_lock, self.base_files, **kw)

    def release(self, srv, lock):
        return Release(srv.lock(lock), retries=1, backoff=0)

    def read(self, path):
        with open(path, "rb") as f:
            return f.read()

    def test_a_file_the_patch_lists_comes_from_the_patch_zip_and_the_rest_from_the_base(self):
        lock, patch_files = self.patched()
        with fx.RangeServer(self.dir) as srv:
            rel = self.release(srv, lock)
            for name in (QUESTS, GUIDES, POCKETS):
                self.assertEqual(rel.extract(name, os.path.join(self.dir, "out", name)), "ok")
            self.assertEqual(rel.extract(QUESTS, os.path.join(self.dir, "out", QUESTS)), "skip")
            self.assertIn(f"/{fx.PATCH_NAME}", {path for path, _ in srv.requests})
        self.assertEqual(self.read(os.path.join(self.dir, "out", QUESTS)), patch_files[QUESTS])
        self.assertEqual(self.read(os.path.join(self.dir, "out", POCKETS)), patch_files[POCKETS])
        self.assertEqual(self.read(os.path.join(self.dir, "out", GUIDES)), self.base_files[GUIDES])

    def test_a_patch_file_not_matching_patch_json_is_refused_and_not_left_behind(self):
        lock, _ = self.patched(tamper={QUESTS: "1" * 64})
        dest = os.path.join(self.dir, "out", "classic-quests.json")
        with fx.RangeServer(self.dir) as srv:
            with self.assertRaisesRegex(FetchError, "SHA-256 mismatch against the patch's patch.json"):
                self.release(srv, lock).extract(QUESTS, dest)
        self.assertFalse(os.path.exists(dest))
        self.assertFalse(os.path.exists(dest + ".part"))

    def test_patch_json_not_matching_the_lock_pin_is_refused(self):
        lock, _ = self.patched()
        lock["patch"]["patch_json_sha256"] = "0" * 64
        with fx.RangeServer(self.dir) as srv:
            with self.assertRaisesRegex(FetchError, "SHA-256 mismatch against the lock's pin: patch.json"):
                self.release(srv, lock).extract(GUIDES, os.path.join(self.dir, "out", "guides.json"))

    def test_a_patch_for_another_base_release_is_refused(self):
        lock, _ = self.patched(identity_from=["2" * 64])
        with fx.RangeServer(self.dir) as srv:
            with self.assertRaisesRegex(FetchError, "is not for this base release"):
                self.release(srv, lock).files_under("runtime/server/")

    def test_files_under_has_the_patchs_new_files_and_not_its_removed_ones(self):
        lock, _ = self.patched(remove=[NAV2])
        with fx.RangeServer(self.dir) as srv:
            rel = self.release(srv, lock)
            self.assertEqual(rel.files_under("runtime/server/navmesh/"), [POCKETS, "runtime/server/navmesh/zone001.nav"])
            with self.assertRaisesRegex(FetchError, "removed by the test patch"):
                rel.extract(NAV2, os.path.join(self.dir, "out", "zone002.nav"))
        self.assertFalse(os.path.exists(os.path.join(self.dir, "out", "zone002.nav")))

    def test_edition_files_come_from_the_base_though_the_patch_removes_them(self):
        lock, _ = self.patched(remove=[CLASSIC_DLL, CLASSIC_DB])
        dest = os.path.join(self.dir, "out", "game.dll")
        with fx.RangeServer(self.dir) as srv:
            rel = self.release(srv, lock)
            self.assertIn(CLASSIC_DLL, rel.files_under("editions/"))
            self.assertEqual(rel.extract(CLASSIC_DLL, dest), "ok")
        self.assertEqual(self.read(dest), self.base_files[CLASSIC_DLL])

    def test_a_patch_that_ships_an_edition_file_is_refused(self):
        lock, _ = self.patched(files={CLASSIC_DLL: b"patched game.dll"})
        with fx.RangeServer(self.dir) as srv:
            with self.assertRaisesRegex(FetchError, "only the base may hold"):
                self.release(srv, lock).extract(CLASSIC_DLL, os.path.join(self.dir, "out", "game.dll"))

    def test_the_world_update_is_the_file_both_the_lock_and_patch_json_name(self):
        update = dict(UPDATE, Tables=[{"Table": "t", "Columns": ["id"], "Key": ["id"], "IgnoreCompare": [],
                                       "Insert": [[1]], "Update": [], "Delete": []}])
        lock, _ = self.patched(updates={"classic": ("plain", "db-test.json", update)})
        with fx.RangeServer(self.dir) as srv:
            self.assertEqual(self.release(srv, lock).world_update("classic"), ("test.1", update))
            with self.assertRaisesRegex(FetchError, "names no world update .* for edition 'b'"):
                self.release(srv, lock).world_update("b")
            other = json.loads(json.dumps(lock))
            other["patch"]["world_updates"]["classic"]["database"] = "b"
            with self.assertRaisesRegex(FetchError, "patch.json names None as the 'b' world update"):
                self.release(srv, other).world_update("classic")
            other = json.loads(json.dumps(lock))
            other["patch"]["world_updates"]["classic"]["sha256"] = "3" * 64
            with self.assertRaisesRegex(FetchError, "SHA-256 mismatch against the lock's pin: db-test.json"):
                self.release(srv, other).world_update("classic")

    def test_a_release_without_a_patch_has_no_world_update(self):
        with fx.RangeServer(self.dir) as srv:
            rel = self.release(srv, self.base_lock)
            self.assertIsNone(rel.world_update("classic"))
            self.assertIsNone(rel.patch())

    def test_fetch_client_takes_a_client_file_the_patch_replaces(self):
        paths = "runtime/client-opendaoc/app/paths.dat"
        lock, patch_files = self.patched(files={paths: b"[paths]\r\nsettings=OfflineDAoC037"})
        client = os.path.join(self.dir, "client")
        os.makedirs(client)
        with open(os.path.join(client, "paths.dat"), "wb") as f:

        self.assertEqual(self.read(os.path.join(client, "game.dll")), self.base_files[CLASSIC_DLL])

    def test_cli_list_shows_each_file_from_where_it_comes(self):

                                                   f"{7000:>12} {NAV2}"])
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`Archive` takes over the range reads, the central directory and the checked extraction; `Release` adds the
manifest, the patch and the resolution. The patch zip is read by range requests like a one-part release (its
central directory, `patch.json` and the few files needed, about 0.5 MB for 0.37), not downloaded whole.

```bash
patch.json and its world updates. A file patch.json lists comes from the patch zip, checked
against patch.json; a file it removes is gone; every other file comes from the base, checked
against the base's manifest. Files under editions/ always come from the base: upstream's
patches never ship them, and remove them from installed games only because they are used while
installing the base (make_patch.py GAME_SKIP_PREFIXES). The patch must be for this base: the
base's hash of patch.json's Identity file is one of the hashes the patch says it updates.

class Release(Archive):
    """The release the lock pins: its base archive, and its patch when the lock has one.

    manifest_file: a local copy of the base's package manifest (upstream commits them, e.g.
    source/tools/release-baselines/0.36/0.36b-package.sha256), checked against the same pin, used
    instead of the one in the archive."""

    def __init__(self, lock, opener=None, retries=4, backoff=1.0, manifest_file=None):
        super().__init__([p["url"] for p in lock["parts"]], [p["size"] for p in lock["parts"]],
                         lock["root_folder"], f"release {lock['version']}", opener, retries, backoff)
        self.lock = lock
        self.version = lock["version"]
        self.manifest_path = lock.get("manifest_path", "PACKAGE MANIFEST.sha256")
        self.manifest_sha256 = lock["manifest_sha256"].lower()
        self.manifest_file = manifest_file
        self._manifest = None
        self._patch = None

    @classmethod
    def from_lock_file(cls, path, **kwargs):
        with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
            return cls(json.load(f), **kwargs)

    def manifest(self):
        """{relative path: sha256}, after checking the manifest against the pinned hash."""
        if self._manifest is not None:
            return self._manifest
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            path = self.manifest_file or os.path.join(tmp, "manifest")
            if not self.manifest_file:
                self._extract_entry(self.manifest_path, path, None)
            got = sha256_file(path)
            if got != self.manifest_sha256:
                raise FetchError(f"{self.manifest_path} SHA-256 {got} does not match the pinned {self.manifest_sha256}")
            sums = {}
            with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
                for line in f:
                    line = line.rstrip("\r\n")
                    if len(line) > 66 and line[64:66] == "  ":
                        sums[line[66:]] = line[:64].lower()
        self._manifest = sums
        return sums

    # ---- the patch (lock "patch") ----
    def patch(self):
        """None without a patch, else {"meta": patch.json, "zip": the patch Archive, "files": {rel: patch.json
        entry}, "removed": {rels}} once patch.json matched the lock's pin and the patch is for this base."""
        if self._patch is not None or not self.lock.get("patch"):
            return self._patch
        p = self.lock["patch"]
        zip_ = Archive([p["url"]], [p["size"]], p["root_folder"], f"the {self.version} patch",
                       self._open, self.retries, self.backoff)
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            path = os.path.join(tmp, "patch.json")
            zip_._extract_entry("patch.json", path, p["patch_json_sha256"].lower(), "the lock's pin")
            with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
                meta = json.load(f, parse_constant=_no_constants)
        files = {}
        for f in meta["Files"]:
            rel = safe_rel(f["Path"])
            if rel.startswith(BASE_ONLY_PREFIXES):
                raise FetchError(f"the {self.version} patch ships {rel}, which only the base may hold")
            files[rel] = f
        removed = {safe_rel(r["Path"]) for r in meta["Remove"]}
        removed = {r for r in removed if not r.startswith(BASE_ONLY_PREFIXES)}
        ident = meta["Identity"]
        if self.manifest().get(ident["Path"]) not in [h.lower() for h in ident["From"]]:
            raise FetchError(f"the {self.version} patch is not for this base release: its {ident['Path']} "
                             "is not one the patch updates")
        self._patch = {"meta": meta, "zip": zip_, "files": files, "removed": removed}
        return self._patch

    def _patch_file(self, rel):
        """The patch.json entry of rel when rel comes from the patch, else None; FetchError when it removes rel."""
        patch = self.patch()
        if patch is None or rel.startswith(BASE_ONLY_PREFIXES):
            return None
        if rel in patch["removed"]:
            raise FetchError(f"{rel} is removed by the {self.version} patch")
        return patch["files"].get(rel)

    def world_update(self, edition):
        """None without a patch, else (the patch's version, the parsed world update for `edition`): the file the
        lock names for it, which patch.json's Database map must name too, checked against the lock's SHA-256."""
        self.edition(edition)
        patch = self.patch()
        if patch is None:
            return None
        wu = self.lock["patch"]["world_updates"].get(edition)
        if wu is None:
            raise FetchError(f"the lock names no world update of the {self.version} patch for edition {edition!r}")
        named = patch["meta"]["Database"].get(wu["database"])
        if named != wu["path"]:
            raise FetchError(f"patch.json names {named!r} as the {wu['database']!r} world update, the lock {wu['path']!r}")
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            path = os.path.join(tmp, "update.json")
            patch["zip"]._extract_entry(safe_rel(wu["path"]), path, wu["sha256"].lower(), "the lock's pin")
            with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
                update = json.load(f, parse_constant=_no_constants)
        return patch["meta"]["Version"], update

    # ---- files of the release (the patch's where it has them) ----
    def entry(self, rel):
        """The ZIP entry (size, CRC32, ...) rel is read from."""
        f = self._patch_file(rel)
        e = self._patch["zip"].entries().get("files/" + rel) if f else self.entries().get(rel)
        if e is None:
            raise FetchError(f"not in release {self.version}: {rel}")
        return e

    def expected_sha256(self, rel):
        f = self._patch_file(rel)
        if f is not None:
            return f["Sha256"].lower()
        sha = self.manifest().get(rel)
```

```bash
LC_ALL=C git add tools/linux/odaoc_fetch.py tools/linux/tests/release_fixture.py tools/linux/tests/test_odaoc_fetch.py client/tests/test_setup.py
LC_ALL=C git commit -F - <<'MSG'
feat(sync): fetch a release made of a base and an upstream patch

Upstream ships 0.37b as the full 0.36b game plus a game patch zip. A lock
may now carry a "patch" object: the fetcher reads the patch zip by range
requests like the base parts, checks its patch.json against the lock's
pin and checks that the patch is for this base (its Identity file). A
file patch.json lists comes from the patch zip, checked against
patch.json; a file it removes is gone; editions/ and every other file
come from the base, checked against the base's manifest. world_update()
returns the patch's world update for an edition, checked against the
lock. The base's manifest may also be read from a local copy, checked
against the same pin. The client bundle carries this fetcher, so
setup.sh reads such a lock too.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
MSG
```

### Task 6: World update applier (upstream's patcher, strict)

**Files:**
- Create: `deploy/bin/world_update.py`
- Test: `deploy/tests/test_world_update.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `odaoc_fetch.FetchError` (Task 5 leaves it unchanged).
- Produces: `deploy/bin/world_update.py` (mode 100644, a module like `epic_chains.py`): `class
  WorldUpdateError(FetchError)`; `value(v)` (a JSON value as Program.cs `Value()` binds it); `apply(db, update,
  version, log=print)` returning `{"added": n, "changed": n, "removed": n}`, or None when `offline_patch_log`
  already has `version`. The log table is `offline_patch_log (Version TEXT PRIMARY KEY, AppliedUtc TEXT, Notes
  TEXT)`, AppliedUtc in .NET's "o" format (7 fractional digits, `Z`), Notes `""`. Log lines: `World data updated to
  <version>: <a> added, <c> changed, <r> removed.` and `World data: already updated to <version>.`. Refusals (the
  file is left byte for byte as it was): `the <version> world update does not fit this world: <n> of its rows did not
  apply (<Table> <insert|update|delete> <Key>=<value>, ...); the world is unchanged` and `the <version> world update
  could not be applied (<cause>); the world is unchanged`.

The port follows `/home/farral/Games/DAoC/research/upstream-037-2026-10-18/sync-data/worlds-scripts/apply_world_update.py` (itself a port of `Program.cs:264-396@v0.37b`) with two
differences, both for "refuse and leave the world unchanged": `offline_patch_log` is created inside the transaction
(Program.cs creates it before), and a world that already has the log row is not written at all.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```bash
mkdir -p deploy/tests
cat > deploy/tests/test_world_update.py <<'HDC_EOF'
"""deploy/bin/world_update.py: upstream's world update (db-<version>.json of a game patch), applied as upstream's
patcher applies it (OfflineDaoc.Patcher/Program.cs WorldUpdate at v0.37b), but strict like upstream's own check of a
new patch (make_patch.py check_world_update): a row that does not apply leaves the world as it was."""
import copy
import hashlib
import os
import sqlite3
import sys
import tempfile
import unittest

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
REPO = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(HERE, "..", ".."))
sys.path[:0] = [os.path.join(REPO, "deploy", "bin"), os.path.join(REPO, "tools", "linux"),
                os.path.join(REPO, "tools", "linux", "tests")]

import world_update  # noqa: E402
from odaoc_fetch import FetchError  # noqa: E402

WORLD = """
CREATE TABLE Mob (Mob_ID TEXT PRIMARY KEY, Name TEXT, Level INTEGER, Size INTEGER, Note TEXT, LastTimeRowUpdated TEXT);
INSERT INTO Mob VALUES ('m1', 'pooka', 10, 30, NULL, '2026-01-01');
INSERT INTO Mob VALUES ('m2', 'blackthorn', 20, 40, 'x', '2026-01-01');
INSERT INTO Mob VALUES ('m3', 'falling ice', 5, 10, NULL, '2026-01-01');
CREATE TABLE ItemTemplate (Id_nb TEXT PRIMARY KEY, Model INTEGER, Icon BLOB, Flag INTEGER, Price REAL);
INSERT INTO ItemTemplate VALUES ('sword', 1, X'00ff', 0, 1.5);
"""
MOB = ["Mob_ID", "Name", "Level", "Size", "Note", "LastTimeRowUpdated"]
UPDATE = {
    "Version": "0.37",
    "IgnoreColumns": ["lasttimerowupdated"],  # names compare without case, as in Program.cs
    "NewTables": [{"Table": "offline_world_bot_guild",
                   "Create": "CREATE TABLE offline_world_bot_guild (BotId TEXT PRIMARY KEY, GuildID TEXT)",
                   "Indexes": ["CREATE INDEX idx_bot_guild ON offline_world_bot_guild (GuildID)"]}],
    "NewColumns": [{"Table": "Mob", "Column": "PathID", "Decl": "TEXT"}],
    "Tables": [
        {"Table": "Mob", "Columns": MOB, "Key": ["Mob_ID"], "IgnoreCompare": [],
         "Insert": [["m4", "fenrir shredder", 30, 50, None, "2026-10-01"]],
         # The old LastTimeRowUpdated differs from the world's: an ignored column. Note is NULL: compared with IS.
         "Update": [{"Old": ["m1", "pooka", 10, 30, None, "2025-12-31"], "New": ["m1", "pooka", 11, 35, None, "2026-10-01"]}],
         "Delete": [["m3", "falling ice", 5, 10, None, "2026-01-01"]]},
        {"Table": "ItemTemplate", "Columns": ["Id_nb", "Model", "Icon", "Flag", "Price"], "Key": ["Id_nb"],
         "IgnoreCompare": ["Price"],  # this table's own ignored column
         "Insert": [],
         "Update": [{"Old": ["sword", 1, {"$b64": "AP8="}, False, 9.9], "New": ["sword", 2, {"$b64": "AQI="}, True, 2.5]}],
         "Delete": []},
    ],
}
QUIET = lambda *a, **k: None  # noqa: E731


def sha(path):
    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        return hashlib.sha256(f.read()).hexdigest()


def rows(db, sql):
    con = sqlite3.connect(f"file:{db}?mode=ro", uri=True)
    try:
        return con.execute(sql).fetchall()
    finally:
        con.close()


class ApplyTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def setUp(self):
        self.tmp = tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()
        self.db = os.path.join(self.tmp.name, "world.db")
        con = sqlite3.connect(self.db)
        con.executescript(WORLD)
        con.close()

    def tearDown(self):
        self.tmp.cleanup()

    def test_it_adds_the_new_tables_and_columns_and_applies_every_row_with_a_log_row(self):
        logged = []
        self.assertEqual(world_update.apply(self.db, UPDATE, "0.37", log=logged.append),
                         {"added": 1, "changed": 2, "removed": 1})
        self.assertEqual(logged, ["World data updated to 0.37: 1 added, 2 changed, 1 removed."])
        self.assertEqual(rows(self.db, "SELECT Mob_ID, Name, Level, Size, LastTimeRowUpdated, PathID FROM Mob ORDER BY Mob_ID"),
                         [("m1", "pooka", 11, 35, "2026-10-01", None), ("m2", "blackthorn", 20, 40, "2026-01-01", None),
                          ("m4", "fenrir shredder", 30, 50, "2026-10-01", None)])
        self.assertEqual(rows(self.db, "SELECT * FROM ItemTemplate"), [("sword", 2, b"\x01\x02", 1, 2.5)])
        self.assertEqual(rows(self.db, "SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE tbl_name='offline_world_bot_guild' ORDER BY name"),
                         [("idx_bot_guild",), ("offline_world_bot_guild",), ("sqlite_autoindex_offline_world_bot_guild_1",)])
        (version, applied, notes), = rows(self.db, "SELECT Version, AppliedUtc, Notes FROM offline_patch_log")
        self.assertEqual((version, notes), ("0.37", ""))
        self.assertRegex(applied, r"^\d{4}-\d\d-\d\dT\d\d:\d\d:\d\d\.\d{7}Z$")  # .NET's "o" format, as Program.cs writes it

    def test_a_second_run_changes_nothing(self):
        world_update.apply(self.db, UPDATE, "0.37", log=QUIET)
        before = sha(self.db)
        logged = []
        self.assertIsNone(world_update.apply(self.db, UPDATE, "0.37", log=logged.append))
        self.assertEqual(logged, ["World data: already updated to 0.37."])
        self.assertEqual(sha(self.db), before)

    def refused(self, update, pattern):
        before = sha(self.db)
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(world_update.WorldUpdateError, pattern):
            world_update.apply(self.db, update, "0.37", log=QUIET)
        self.assertEqual(sha(self.db), before)  # byte for byte: no new table, column or log row either

    def test_a_row_whose_old_value_is_gone_is_refused_and_the_world_is_left_as_it_was(self):
        con = sqlite3.connect(self.db)
        con.execute("UPDATE Mob SET Level=12 WHERE Mob_ID='m1'")  # the game (or a fix) changed it
        con.commit()
        con.close()
        self.refused(UPDATE, r"^the 0\.37 world update does not fit this world: 1 of its rows did not apply "
                             r"\(Mob update Mob_ID='m1'\); the world is unchanged$")

    def test_an_added_row_that_is_there_already_is_refused(self):
        con = sqlite3.connect(self.db)
        con.execute("INSERT INTO Mob VALUES ('m4', 'fenrir shredder', 30, 50, NULL, '2026-10-01')")
        con.commit()
        con.close()
        self.refused(UPDATE, r"1 of its rows did not apply \(Mob insert Mob_ID='m4'\)")

    def test_a_removed_row_that_is_gone_already_is_refused(self):
        update = copy.deepcopy(UPDATE)
        update["Tables"][0]["Delete"].append(["m9", "gone", 1, 1, None, "x"])
        update["Tables"][1]["Update"][0]["Old"][1] = 7
        self.refused(update, r"2 of its rows did not apply \(Mob delete Mob_ID='m9', ItemTemplate update Id_nb='sword'\)")

    def test_an_unexpected_value_is_refused(self):
        update = copy.deepcopy(UPDATE)
        update["Tables"][0]["Insert"][0][4] = [1]
        self.refused(update, r"could not be applied \(unexpected value in the world update: \[1\]\)")

    def test_a_table_the_world_lacks_is_refused(self):
        update = copy.deepcopy(UPDATE)
        update["Tables"].append({"Table": "Nope", "Columns": ["a"], "Key": ["a"], "Insert": [[1]], "Update": [], "Delete": []})
        self.refused(update, r"could not be applied \(no such table: Nope\)")

    def test_tables_and_columns_the_world_has_already_are_kept(self):
        con = sqlite3.connect(self.db)
        con.executescript("CREATE TABLE offline_world_bot_guild (BotId TEXT PRIMARY KEY, GuildID TEXT);"
                          "ALTER TABLE Mob ADD COLUMN pathid TEXT;")
        con.close()
        world_update.apply(self.db, UPDATE, "0.37", log=QUIET)
        self.assertEqual([r[1] for r in rows(self.db, "PRAGMA table_info(Mob)")], MOB + ["pathid"])

    def test_numbers_decode_as_the_patcher_reads_them(self):
        self.assertEqual(world_update.value(2 ** 63 - 1), 2 ** 63 - 1)
        self.assertEqual(world_update.value(2 ** 63), float(2 ** 63))  # past int64: a double, as TryGetInt64 fails
        self.assertEqual((world_update.value(True), world_update.value(False)), (1, 0))
        self.assertEqual(world_update.value({"$b64": "AP8="}), b"\x00\xff")
        self.assertIsNone(world_update.value(None))

    def test_a_refusal_is_a_fetch_error(self):
        # init_world and world_admin report it like a failed download: the start or the command stops.
        self.assertTrue(issubclass(world_update.WorldUpdateError, FetchError))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
HDC_EOF
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

```bash
mkdir -p deploy/bin
cat > deploy/bin/world_update.py <<'HDC_EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Upstream's world update (db-<version>.json in an OfflineDAoC game patch), applied to a world database.

A release upstream ships as a patch on a full base release (0.37b: the 0.36b game plus the 0.37 game patch) has no
world file of its own: its clean world is the base's clean world plus the patch's world update, a JSON row diff. This
applies it as upstream's patcher does (source/tools/OfflineDaoc.Patcher/Program.cs, class WorldUpdate, lines 264-396
at v0.37b):
- new tables and their indexes (CREATE ... IF NOT EXISTS), and new columns where the table lacks them (names compared
  without case);
- per table: INSERT OR IGNORE of the added rows; UPDATE ... SET every column WHERE every compared column IS its old
  value; DELETE ... WHERE the same; compared = every column but the update's IgnoreColumns and the table's
  IgnoreCompare (names without case);
- values as Program.cs Value() reads them: null, text, integers (int64, past it a double), doubles, true and false as
  1 and 0, {"$b64": ...} as a blob;
- all in one transaction with the offline_patch_log row (Version, AppliedUtc in .NET's "o" format, Notes), so the
  update runs once, and upstream's patcher, should it ever see this world, knows the update is in.
Unlike the patcher, which leaves a row "as the game had it" when it does not match, this is strict like upstream's
check of a new patch against the released base (source/tools/make_patch.py check_world_update, `assert kept == 0`,
line 189): any row that does not apply rolls everything back and raises WorldUpdateError naming the rows. The log
table is created inside the transaction too, so a refused update leaves the file byte for byte as it was. A world
that already has the log row is left alone, so a second run changes nothing. Nothing in the patch is executed.
"""
import base64
import datetime
import os
import re
import sqlite3
import sys

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
sys.path[:0] = [HERE, os.path.join(HERE, "..", "..", "tools", "linux")]

from odaoc_fetch import FetchError  # noqa: E402

INT64_MIN, INT64_MAX = -(1 << 63), (1 << 63) - 1


class WorldUpdateError(FetchError):
    """The world update does not fit the world; the world is unchanged."""


def _q(name):
    return '"' + name.replace('"', '""') + '"'


def _if_not_exists(sql):
    # Program.cs Exec(..., ignoreExists: true): three case-insensitive replacements, in this order.
    for old, new in (("CREATE TABLE ", "CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS "), ("CREATE INDEX ", "CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS "),
                     ("CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ", "CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ")):
        sql = re.sub(re.escape(old), lambda m, new=new: new, sql, flags=re.IGNORECASE)
    return sql


def value(v):
    """A JSON value of the update as the SQLite value Program.cs WorldUpdate.Value() binds."""
    if v is None or isinstance(v, (str, float)):
        return v
    if isinstance(v, bool):
        return 1 if v else 0
    if isinstance(v, int):
        return v if INT64_MIN <= v <= INT64_MAX else float(v)
    if isinstance(v, dict) and set(v) == {"$b64"}:
        return base64.b64decode(v["$b64"], validate=True)
    raise ValueError(f"unexpected value in the world update: {v!r}")


def _applied(con, version):
    if con.execute("SELECT count(*) FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' AND lower(name)='offline_patch_log'").fetchone()[0] == 0:
        return False
    return con.execute("SELECT count(*) FROM offline_patch_log WHERE Version=?", (version,)).fetchone()[0] > 0


def _rows(con, update):
    """Apply the update's tables, columns and rows; returns (counts, the rows that did not apply)."""
    con.execute("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS offline_patch_log (Version TEXT PRIMARY KEY, AppliedUtc TEXT, Notes TEXT)")
    for t in update["NewTables"]:
        con.execute(_if_not_exists(t["Create"]))
        for index in t.get("Indexes", []):
            con.execute(_if_not_exists(index))
    for col in update["NewColumns"]:
        have = {r[1].lower() for r in con.execute(f"PRAGMA table_info({_q(col['Table'])})")}
        if col["Column"].lower() not in have:
            con.execute(f"ALTER TABLE {_q(col['Table'])} ADD COLUMN {_q(col['Column'])} {col['Decl']}")
    ignored = {c.lower() for c in update["IgnoreColumns"]}
    counts, missed = {"added": 0, "changed": 0, "removed": 0}, []
    for t in update["Tables"]:
        table, cols = t["Table"], t["Columns"]
        skip = ignored | {c.lower() for c in t.get("IgnoreCompare", [])}
        cmp = [i for i, c in enumerate(cols) if c.lower() not in skip]
        where = " AND ".join(f"{_q(cols[i])} IS ?" for i in cmp)
        key = [cols.index(k) for k in t["Key"]]

        def miss(op, row):
            missed.append(f"{table} {op} " + ", ".join(f"{cols[i]}={row[i]!r}" for i in key))

        insert = f"INSERT OR IGNORE INTO {_q(table)} ({','.join(_q(c) for c in cols)}) VALUES ({','.join('?' * len(cols))})"
        for row in t["Insert"]:
            if con.execute(insert, [value(v) for v in row]).rowcount == 1:
                counts["added"] += 1
            else:
                miss("insert", row)
        change = f"UPDATE {_q(table)} SET {','.join(_q(c) + '=?' for c in cols)} WHERE {where}"
        for row in t["Update"]:
            if con.execute(change, [value(v) for v in row["New"]] + [value(row["Old"][i]) for i in cmp]).rowcount > 0:
                counts["changed"] += 1
            else:
                miss("update", row["Old"])
        remove = f"DELETE FROM {_q(table)} WHERE {where}"
        for row in t["Delete"]:
            if con.execute(remove, [value(row[i]) for i in cmp]).rowcount > 0:
                counts["removed"] += 1
            else:
                miss("delete", row)
    return counts, missed


def apply(db, update, version, log=print):
    """Apply `update` (a parsed db-<version>.json) to the world database `db` as patch `version`. Returns
    {"added", "changed", "removed"} counts, or None when the world already has this version's update. Raises
    WorldUpdateError, with the world unchanged, when any row does not apply or the update cannot be applied."""
    con = sqlite3.connect(db, isolation_level=None)  # autocommit; the one transaction is begun and ended here
    try:
        if _applied(con, version):
            log(f"World data: already updated to {version}.")
            return None
        con.execute("BEGIN")
        try:
            counts, missed = _rows(con, update)
            if missed:
                shown = ", ".join(missed[:10]) + (f", and {len(missed) - 10} more" if len(missed) > 10 else "")
                raise WorldUpdateError(f"the {version} world update does not fit this world: {len(missed)} of its rows "
                                       f"did not apply ({shown}); the world is unchanged")
            now = datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc)
            applied_utc = now.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S.") + f"{now.microsecond:06d}0Z"  # .NET DateTime "o"
            con.execute("INSERT INTO offline_patch_log (Version, AppliedUtc, Notes) VALUES (?, ?, ?)",
                        (version, applied_utc, ""))
            con.execute("COMMIT")
        except BaseException as e:
            if con.in_transaction:
                con.execute("ROLLBACK")
            if isinstance(e, (sqlite3.Error, KeyError, TypeError, ValueError)):
                raise WorldUpdateError(f"the {version} world update could not be applied ({e}); "
                                       "the world is unchanged") from e
            raise
    finally:
        con.close()
    log(f"World data updated to {version}: {counts['added']} added, {counts['changed']} changed, "
        f"{counts['removed']} removed.")
    return counts
HDC_EOF
```

```bash
feat(sync): apply upstream's world update strictly, in one transaction

world_update.apply() is a port of upstream's patcher (Program.cs
WorldUpdate at v0.37b): new tables and columns, INSERT OR IGNORE, UPDATE
and DELETE where every compared column IS its old value, and the
offline_patch_log row, all in one transaction. It is strict like
make_patch.py's own check: a row that does not apply rolls everything
back and names the rows, so the world stays byte for byte as it was. A
world that already has the log row is left alone. The error is a
FetchError, so the start and the world commands stop on it as on a
failed download.
```

  is built as `<dest>.building` and replaces dest only after every row applied. `init_world.world_cache_dir(data)` =
  `<data>/upstream-cache` (also in `not_writable_hint`), `init_world.prune_world_cache(data, release)` (run by
  `init_world.main` after a good start, beside `prune_server_files`). `init_world.init` (new world),
  `world_admin.fetch_clean` and `world_admin.upgrade_world` (without `--clean-world`) build through it. Fixture:

  clean classic world for tests (database only)`; its command is unchanged, so `HDC_TEST_WORLD` is the patched world
  and `server-files/<version>/classic-quests.json` beside it is the patch's.

```bash
    upstream's 0.37 patch is: a new classic-quests.json and navmesh/pockets.json, and a world update for both

    return lock, files, patch_files
```

```bash
class BuildWorldTests(unittest.TestCase):
    """build_world: a release's clean world for an edition, the base's world plus the patch's world update."""

    def setUp(self):
        self.tmp = tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()
        self.dir = self.tmp.name
        self.dest = os.path.join(self.dir, "data", "world", "opendaoc.sqlite3.db")
        self.cache = os.path.join(self.dir, "data", "upstream-cache")
        self.cached = os.path.join(self.cache, fx.ROOT.rstrip("/"), CLASSIC_DB)

    def tearDown(self):
        self.tmp.cleanup()

    def build(self, srv, lock, log=QUIET):
        return world_update.build_world(Release(srv.lock(lock), retries=1, backoff=0), "classic", self.dest,
                                        self.cache, log)

    def test_without_a_patch_the_world_is_the_base_world_and_nothing_is_cached(self):
        lock, files = fx.build(self.dir)
        with fx.RangeServer(self.dir) as srv:
            self.assertEqual(self.build(srv, lock), "ok")
            self.assertEqual(self.build(srv, lock), "skip")
        with open(self.dest, "rb") as f:
            self.assertEqual(f.read(), files[CLASSIC_DB])
        self.assertFalse(os.path.exists(self.cache))

    def test_with_a_patch_the_verified_base_world_is_cached_and_a_copy_gets_the_update(self):
        lock, files, _ = fx.build_patched(self.dir)
        logged = []
        with fx.RangeServer(self.dir) as srv:
            self.assertEqual(self.build(srv, lock, logged.append), "ok")
        self.assertEqual(logged, ["World data updated to test.1: 1 added, 1 changed, 0 removed."])
        self.assertEqual(rows(self.dest, "SELECT Mob_ID, Level FROM Mob ORDER BY Mob_ID"), [("m1", 11), ("m2", 30)])
        self.assertEqual(rows(self.dest, "SELECT Version, Notes FROM offline_patch_log"), [("test.1", "")])
        with open(self.cached, "rb") as f:
            self.assertEqual(f.read(), files[CLASSIC_DB])
        self.assertEqual(os.listdir(os.path.dirname(self.dest)), ["opendaoc.sqlite3.db"])  # no .building or journal

    def test_the_next_build_downloads_no_world_and_rebuilds_the_copy_from_the_cache(self):
        lock, _, _ = fx.build_patched(self.dir)
        with fx.RangeServer(self.dir) as srv:
            self.build(srv, lock)
            cached = os.stat(self.cached).st_mtime_ns
            with open(self.dest, "r+b") as f:
                f.write(b"damaged")  # whatever is at dest is replaced
            self.assertEqual(self.build(srv, lock), "skip")
        self.assertEqual(os.stat(self.cached).st_mtime_ns, cached)
        self.assertEqual(rows(self.dest, "SELECT Mob_ID, Level FROM Mob ORDER BY Mob_ID"), [("m1", 11), ("m2", 30)])

    def test_an_update_that_does_not_fit_the_base_leaves_dest_as_it_was(self):
        lock, _, _ = fx.build_patched(self.dir, update=fx.world_update(old_level=9))
        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(self.dest))
        with open(self.dest, "wb") as f:
            f.write(b"the world before")

            with self.assertRaisesRegex(world_update.WorldUpdateError, r"1 of its rows did not apply \(Mob update Mob_ID='m1'\)"):
                self.build(srv, lock)
        with open(self.dest, "rb") as f:
            self.assertEqual(f.read(), b"the world before")
        self.assertEqual(os.listdir(os.path.dirname(self.dest)), ["opendaoc.sqlite3.db"])

    def test_a_base_world_not_matching_the_manifest_is_refused_before_anything_is_built(self):
        files = dict(fx.DEFAULT_FILES, **{CLASSIC_DB: fx.sqlite_world(*fx.WORLD_SQL)})
        lock, files = fx.build(self.dir, files, tamper={CLASSIC_DB: "4" * 64})
        lock, _ = fx.build_patch(self.dir, lock, files, updates={"classic": ("plain", "db.json", fx.world_update())})
        with fx.RangeServer(self.dir) as srv:
            with self.assertRaisesRegex(FetchError, "SHA-256 mismatch against the release manifest"):
                self.build(srv, lock)
        self.assertFalse(os.path.exists(self.dest))
        self.assertFalse(os.path.exists(self.cached))
```
